# Governance Identifier Ledger

- **Tier:** governance metadata (non-normative as to product or architecture)
- **Status:** ACTIVE
- **Authority:** Product Board, **PBD-0005** — Governance Identifier Ledger
  Establishment and Collision Resolution
- **Established:** 2026-08-13

> **This ledger allocates identifiers. It decides nothing else.**
> It **must not** reinterpret any accepted RFC, ADR, contract, open question, or
> risk, and nothing recorded here changes the meaning, status, or authority of any
> artifact it names. Where this ledger and an accepted document disagree about what
> an artifact *says*, the accepted document governs; this ledger is authoritative
> only for **which number belongs to which artifact**.

---

## 1. Purpose and Authority

No authoritative allocation record was maintained before PBD-0005. Repository
evidence plus the preserved workstream chronology therefore constitute the
historical evidence set, and this ledger is its canonical consolidation.

From this determination forward, the ledger is the **authoritative allocator** for
`PBA`, `PBR`, `ARR`, `ER`, and `PBD` identifiers, and may record other governance
families where useful. No identifier in these namespaces may be issued without
being recorded here first.

## 2. Namespace Rules

| Namespace | Meaning | Issued by |
|---|---|---|
| `PBA` | Product Board Authorization — authorizes a piece of work | Product Board |
| `PBR` | Product Board Review / acceptance record | Product Board |
| `PBD` | Product Board Determination — a governance decision | Product Board |
| `ARR` | Architecture / contract review report | Architecture Reviewer |
| `ER` | Execution report — the record of authorized work performed | Principal Engineer |

Other families appear in the repository as historical artifacts and are recorded in
§9 for reference: `POR` (Product Owner Ratification), `ARW` (architecture working
note), `WN` (workshop note), `WR` (working record), `SCW` (System Context
workshop).

## 3. Immutable Historical-Assignment Rule

A historical assignment is **preserved** and is **never** renumbered merely because
its numerical sequence is non-monotonic.

An assignment may be corrected **only** where:

- **(a)** two distinct artifacts are **affirmatively known** to share one
  identifier; or
- **(b)** an existing repository reference is **affirmatively known** to identify
  the wrong artifact.

**Repository silence is not evidence of a collision.** An identifier absent from
every repository file is not thereby proven free, proven occupied, or proven to be
in conflict.

## 4. No-Gap-Filling Rule

Numbers below a namespace's high-water mark that are absent from the evidence set
**must not** be reused. Every identifier carries exactly one allocation state:

| State | Meaning | May be newly assigned? |
|---|---|---|
| **ALLOCATED** | Bound to a known artifact | No |
| **RESERVED** | Deliberately withheld (e.g. known historical collision) | No |
| **SUPERSEDED-ID** | The artifact's *identifier* was corrected; the artifact itself stands | No |
| **UNKNOWN-HISTORICAL** | Below the high-water mark, disposition unrecoverable | No |
| **AVAILABLE-FUTURE** | Above the high-water mark and unissued | **Yes** |

**Only AVAILABLE-FUTURE identifiers may be newly assigned.**

| **PBA-0118** | **ALLOCATED** | **Phase-5 O-1 Representation-Settlement Determination authorization** → ER-0099 (2026-08-17; Product Board determination, ER-0092 precedent, adopted by the Product Owner. Establishes that **Revision 4 already uniquely determines `declared_values` semantics**: §3.5 specifies "a finite, non-empty set of **distinct** opaque categorical values", §5.1.1 the "value set contains duplicate values" and "value set is empty" violations, and `src/model/evidence.rs` states the `Vec<EnumValue>` is "deliberately so: §5.1 check 6 must be able to detect an **empty** set and **duplicate** values". Therefore `declared_values` is a **set**: order is meaningless and duplicates have no multiplicity; `[A,B]≡[B,A]`, `[A,A]≡[A]`. **DETERMINATION B** — existing contract sufficient; Check 6 implementation must apply set semantics (membership / empty-detection / duplicate-detection over the declared collection) and must **not** use order or duplicate multiplicity. **No `src/model` change; no shared helper required; no Revision 5.** The plan's carried-observation framing ("derived equality is sequence-sensitive … describes a set") is **corrected**: it misstates the contract, which never attributes set semantics to derive a semantic-equality requirement; the model comment and §3.5/§5.1.1 are the authoritative text. O-1 is RECLASSIFIED from DEFERRED-gate to **RESOLVED-BY-DETERMINATION (semantic basis for Check 6)**; Check 6 may now be separately authorized. O-4 remains OPEN/prospective and independent; check 2 frozen) |

| **PBA-0124** | **ALLOCATED** | **ADR-0005 §7.6 interpretation / HashMap authority-boundary determination** → ER-0103 (2026-08-17; Product Board determination under the ER-0092 precedent, adopted by the Product Owner; read-only determination; **no successor revision**. **DETERMINATION A — ADR BEHAVIORAL RULE GOVERNS.** ADR-0005 §7.6 (`docs/adr/0005-slice-1-implementation-language-and-toolchain.md:290–294`) is a **behavioral determinism rule**: it prohibits **nondeterministic influence** from `HashMap`/`HashSet` iteration order on authoritative/conformance-visible results. It does **not** structurally prohibit those collections merely because they participate in authoritative computation. HashMap/HashSet are permitted when their iteration order cannot influence any conformance-visible result — including where the operation is order-independent, or where keys/items are explicitly sorted under the contract's declared orders before an authoritative result is produced (the ADR's "or sort explicitly" permission clause). **Slice-G `src/checks/guard_analysis.rs` satisfies §7.6.** `is_contradictory` uses order-independent existential semantics (`.values().any(...)`); `every_reference` collects lineage keys from HashMaps then **sorts them** (`guard_analysis.rs:283`); `guards_overlap` and `guard_subsumes` inherit deterministic traversal via `every_reference`; `analyze_guard` early-return behavior follows deterministic `Guard.atoms` Vec traversal; no defect membership, multiplicity, anchor, discriminator, or canonical output ordering depends on HashMap iteration order. **HASHMAP GATE CLOSED.** The earlier HASHMAP GATE UNRESOLVED is resolved by this determination. **No Slice-G source remediation required.** Slice-G `guard_analysis.rs` and all Check 9/11/12 implementation files (`direction_conflict.rs`, `unreachable_rules.rs`, `contradictory_rules.rs`) are compliant with ADR-0005 §7.6 as accepted. **Documentation correction completed.** The two over-strict Phase-4 plan wordings (exit-criteria cell and closure narrative) were corrected to the behavioural paraphrase; see commit `066a6be`. **Synchronization.** This cycle records PBA-0124 / ER-0103 and updates the §11 high-water / next-allocatable values; see §11.1. No identifier beyond PBA-0124 / ER-0103 is consumed. ARR-0041 remains reserved for the future independent Slice-G review. Terminates under §12(7).) |

Resolves the two D-classified findings from the Phase-6 read-only discovery (this cycle's baseline `d2133f4`): (1) whether `NotEvaluatedRecord.blocking`'s single-`DefectIdentity` capacity is compatible with a conjunctively-gated unit (14B's four-source prerequisite: 13B + 14A on all three derivations); (2) whether the not-evaluated set requires a §6.4-style canonical total order. **RULING A — root-blocker selection, transitive propagation, canonical-minimum tie-break.** For one suppressed `(check, construct)` evaluation, exactly one `NotEvaluatedRecord` is produced, carrying exactly one root blocking `DefectIdentity`, unchanged from the existing singular field. Blocker candidates: (a) any defect an immediate prerequisite evaluation directly produced; (b) where an immediate prerequisite evaluation was **itself** not-evaluated, its own `NotEvaluatedRecord.blocking` identity is inherited as a candidate — so blocker identity propagates transitively through prerequisite chains (worked example: a Check-2 `UNRESOLVED_REFERENCE` blocks 14A(ENTRY), which is not-evaluated citing that Check-2 defect; 14B's own prerequisite then inherits that same root Check-2 defect as a candidate, never an invented or absent blocker). Where more than one independent root-blocker candidate exists for one suppressed unit, exactly one is selected by the **existing** `DefectIdentity`/`DefectCore` §6.4 canonical order (`Ord`/`try_cmp`, already public, unmodified) — the canonical-minimum candidate. No duplicate `NotEvaluatedRecord` is ever emitted for the same `(check, construct)`. `NotEvaluatedRecord` is **not** changed to hold `Vec<DefectIdentity>`; `src/result/**` is untouched. **RULING B — not-evaluated is set-membership semantics; no new canonical order.** The not-evaluated collection's contract-visible identity is **record membership**, not sequence: obligation 8 (§8) requires reproduction of "the same... not-evaluated set" — the word *set*, not *ordering* — and §6.4's canonical total order is textually scoped to "Canonical **defect** ordering" only (§6.4 heading), never extended to not-evaluated records by any accepted text. No `Ord`, `PartialOrd`, canonical-sort function, or other `src/result/**` API is introduced for `NotEvaluatedRecord`; an implementation may choose any deterministic emission order as a testing convenience without that order being a new §6.4 semantic rule; conformance/reproducibility comparison is by record-membership equality. **Six worked cases verified** (see ER-0109 for full derivations): (1) a direct Check-1→Check-3 block needs no transitivity; (2) Check-2 unresolved-reference → 14A(ENTRY) not-evaluated → 14B not-evaluated, all three citing the one root Check-2 identity by propagation; (3) a direct 13B defect blocks 14B with no transitivity; (4) ENTRY blocked by a Check-2 defect while STOP_LOSS independently carries a real 14A `DIMENSION_MISMATCH` — 14B has two independent root-blocker candidates (the inherited Check-2 identity via 14A(ENTRY), and the direct 14A(STOP_LOSS) defect) and the §6.4 check-number key (`C2 < C14A`) deterministically selects the Check-2 identity, yielding **exactly one** `NotEvaluatedRecord`; (5) a pairwise Check-9 unit blocked by independent defects on each rule of the pair resolves identically via canonical-minimum; (6) one Check-6 declaration defect blocking several distinct `(check, construct)` units yields **several distinct** `NotEvaluatedRecord`s (different construct identities — not duplicates) that all cite the **same** blocking identity, which Rule 6 explicitly permits. **Compatibility confirmed:** both rulings are implementable using only already-public `DefectIdentity::try_cmp`/`is_comparable_with`/`core()` and `DefectCore`'s existing `Ord`; no `src/result/**` change of any kind is required; the ARR-0039 G-1 no-unauthorized-accessor precedent remains intact by construction (nothing is added there); neither ruling contradicts any accepted §5.2/§6.1–§6.4 text — both fill textual silences the discovery correctly identified, not textual conflicts, so **no contract revision is required**. **Post-determination classification: Phase 6 FULLY UNBLOCKED for bounded implementation slicing** — the discovery's contingent 6E (not-evaluated ordering) slice is no longer necessary under Ruling B; the recommended slicing collapses to 6A (reference/dependency enumeration) → 6B (prerequisite evaluation-state + root-blocker propagation, realizing Ruling A) → 6C (fixed-sequence orchestration) → 6D (defect aggregation via the existing `canonical_sort`, `NotEvaluatedRecord` collection via Ruling A/B, verdict assembly via the existing `WellFormednessResult` constructors). **This cycle allocates PBA-0134 / ER-0109 only; it does not allocate implementation identifiers.** Next Phase-6 implementation authorization requires its own PBA/ER pair (PBA-0135 / ER-0110, reserved, not allocated here); independent review requires its own ARR (ARR-0046, reserved, not allocated here). No `src/**` change; docs/governance-only. Terminates under §12(7).) |

(2) obligation 6 (total/pure/deterministic/side-effect-free, §1.1, §8.6) — ALREADY SATISFIED architecturally; PHASE-8 WORK REQUIRED is a documented panic-safety audit of the four production-path `.expect()`/`unreachable!()` sites found in this determination's feasibility audit (`src/result/identity.rs:224`, `src/result/discriminator.rs:283`, `src/checks/finalize.rs:55`, `src/checks/guard_analysis.rs:182` — each currently guarded by an argued-safe internal invariant, not a type-system proof; each is a candidate for either a strengthening regression test proving unreachability under adversarial input, or documentation hardening the invariant argument); (3) obligation 7 (no I/O/persistence/serialization/canonicalization/revision-identity, §8.7) — ALREADY SATISFIED, architecturally enforced, no such dependency exists (ADR-0005); light audit only; (4) obligation 8 — ALREADY SATISFIED per Ruling B, no further work; (5) malformed/adversarial input → UNUSABLE, never crash (Phase-8's own stated test requirement) — PHASE-8 WORK REQUIRED: existing coverage is narrow/targeted (8 conformance tests, single/paired defects); `Strategy::wholly_undeclared` (`src/model/strategy.rs:123`) exists and is exercised in the model layer's own unit tests but is never run end-to-end through `run_phase6`/`finalize_phase6`; extending `tests/conformance/**` (or a sibling test target) with maximally-malformed/structurally-extreme fixtures asserting `Unusable`, never a panic, is the concrete deliverable; (6) determinism/repeated invocation — ALREADY SATISFIED, Phase 7 dimension 15; (7) complete defect/not-evaluated preservation, canonical ordering, prerequisite gating — ALREADY SATISFIED, Phase 7 dimensions 3, 5, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11, 14; (8) full verification gate (`fmt`/`check`/`clippy -D warnings`/`test` green) — standard, required every cycle; (9) `proptest` — see Ruling E; (10) documentation/release-readiness items (e.g. `README.md`'s stale "Foundation phase, no production code exists" framing, absent CI config) — **UNDETERMINED / explicitly out of scope**: Phase 8's own text names no documentation or CI deliverable, and inventing one would exceed textually-supported scope; 1 BLOCKER, 0 MAJOR, 0 MINOR, 0 NOTE; no ARR allocated for the failed re-review, per instruction, matching the PBA-0160/0170/0171 precedent). **Scope — remediation of exactly one BLOCKER, the sole remaining finding.** **BLOCKER (BigInt length-of-length still finite, totality claim false):** the second remediation's tag `0x10` used a single fixed byte `L` (`0 < L ≤ 255`) for the byte-length of the length-of-magnitude value — a genuine improvement in scale over the original 4-byte `u32` ceiling, but structurally the identical defect at a different scale: a fixed-width outer field still caps what is representable, and the ADR's own "architecturally unbounded... for any reason, ever" claim is therefore false, as independent re-review found. **Required remediation direction:** replace the fixed-byte `L` scheme with a **genuinely unbounded canonical length encoding** — preferring a continuation-bit/varint-style unsigned arbitrary-precision length encoding, or another mathematically unbounded, self-delimiting, injective encoding with equivalent properties; the new grammar must contain **no fixed-width outer length field that merely relocates the ceiling again**. **Required properties:** total over every finite non-negative integer; self-delimiting; injective; canonical (exactly one encoding per length value — no redundant/non-canonical representations); independent of platform word size and host endianness; free of leading-zero ambiguity, truncation, and any arbitrary semantic maximum; implementable consistently across languages; must correctly and explicitly cover 0, 1, the 127/128 continuation boundary (if a continuation-bit scheme is chosen), 255/256, values beyond `u32::MAX` and `u64::MAX`, and arbitrarily large finite lengths. **If a continuation-bit varint is selected, the ADR must specify precisely** (not merely say "use varint"): byte order/digit significance; payload bits per byte; continuation-bit meaning; termination rule; canonical shortest representation; whether redundant leading/trailing zero groups are forbidden; zero's unique encoding; decoding-failure behavior for non-canonical representations — no protobuf/LEB128 dependency may be assumed unless the ADR normatively selects and fully pins that external format itself, self-contained. **Tag `0x10` must be re-stated completely** after the new length encoding (sign; canonical magnitude bytes; arbitrary-length magnitude-length prefix; magnitude payload), with a conceptual proof that no finite `BigInt` the accepted model can construct is excluded — not an appeal to "cannot arise in a terminating process." **Rational regression:** tag `0x04` (numerator/denominator, both `0x10` frames) re-confirmed sound under the new prefix — denominator > 0, non-zero, lowest terms, canonical `0/1`, equivalent rationals → identical bytes, distinct rationals → distinct bytes; no unrelated rational redesign. **Error semantics:** remove stale representability-overflow language; ordinary resource exhaustion remains an implementation/runtime concern, never a semantic `RevisionIdentityError` or an artificial Product numeric limit, unless the chosen design actually requires one. **Conformance vectors:** mechanically necessary updates only, adding explicit canonical vectors for the new length grammar — zero, one-byte boundary, multi-byte boundary, >32-bit length, >64-bit length, a very large synthetic length value proven without allocating the corresponding payload, a non-canonical redundant representation explicitly rejected, BigInt framing using the new prefix, rational-framing regression. **Explicitly preserved, not reopened:** Table B; Table D; the complete field/tag registry; the enum semantic registry; BLAKE3 invocation mode; domain separation; `{scheme_version, digest}` `RevisionIdentity`; `OccurrenceId` exclusion; record/collection framing; lineage behavior; RFC-0004 semantics; ADR-0003 classifications; ADR-0007's ruling — only mechanical wording/vector changes the length-grammar replacement itself forces are permitted. **Does NOT authorize:** ADR-0008 acceptance; Matches the **implementation-authorization-only** pattern (cf. **PBA-0168** N-22-B, **PBA-0156** N-22-A) — **no PBR, no ARR, no ER, no PBD this cycle**; the implementation report ER is allocated when the implementation cycle completes, and independent review/closure are separate subsequent cycles. **Architecture hard stop verified:** every implementation decision below is already covered by accepted authority (ADR-0009 §§1–16, RFC-0005) or is an ordinary bounded implementation detail; none requires a new Product/architecture decision, opaque-boundary weakening, semantic-identity change, invented persistence semantics, or reopening accepted authority. **Scope — the minimum implementation of the accepted persistence architecture:** **(A) `ordina` persistence boundary** — new `ordina::persistence` (ADR-0009 §1–§3): `DogfoodEnvelope`, `MintHighWater { Next(u64) | Exhausted }`, `serialize_envelope`, `deserialize_envelope`, fallible `minimum_safe_high_water -> Result<u64, HighWaterExhausted>`, and `EnvelopeLoadError { Malformed, UnsupportedVersion, HighWaterInconsistent, HighWaterExhausted, RationalOutOfDomain }`; the external opaque boundary preserved — any raw-handle transport access stays **`pub(crate)`** exactly as ADR-0009 permits, no public raw-handle accessor. **(B) Full Strategy round-trip** (ADR-0009 §4/§5) — lossless persist/restore of the complete `Strategy`: all six opaque identity domains exact, semantic-model version, evidence declarations, configuration parameters, conditions, direction rules, candidate derivations, `Declared`/`DeclaredCategory` state distinctions, enum/tag distinctions, collection ordering; **no reminting on load**; includes the bounded `pub(crate)`-only `OccurrenceId` transport accessor (ADR-0007 preserved — identity encoder still never walks it). **(C) ExactNumber** (ADR-0009 §4.5) — crate-private (`pub(crate)`) exact reconstruction (e.g. `from_ratio_exact`) with fail-closed decimal-domain validation (strictly-positive denominator; terminating-decimal `2^a·5^b` lowest terms; exact, no float; reject `1/3`, denominator `0`/negative, malformed) accepting huge valid ±terminating decimals and zero; **no public numeric API broadening**. **(D) Mint high-water continuity** (ADR-0009 §6/§7) — `Next(u64)`/`Exhausted`, fallible `minimum_safe_high_water`, **checked** mint advancement (no wrap, no reuse-permitting saturation), `u64::MAX` exhaustion, persisted exhaustion surviving restart, load-time consistency checks (`HighWaterInconsistent`/`HighWaterExhausted`), removed identities never reusable after restart; the single shared occurrence/lineage minter counter preserved. **(E) CheckerContext** (ADR-0009 §8) — persist/restore `DimensionBinding` + supported `VersionToken` exactly; remain caller/Trader-supplied; never manufactured, inferred, or defaulted. **(F) Serialization format/deps** (ADR-0009 §4/§4.4) — JSON via `serde` + `serde_json` over a crate-private DTO with stable explicit enum/tag strings; **numeric-crate `serde` features remain OFF**; the persistence representation must not become a RevisionIdentity canonicalization; the `Cargo.toml` change strictly required by ADR-0009 (add `serde`+`serde_json` to `ordina`, feature set stated explicitly) is in scope. **(G) Load validation / fail-closed** (ADR-0009 §13) — deterministic handling of malformed/truncated input, unsupported version, invalid enum/tag, malformed opaque handle, `RationalOutOfDomain`, `HighWaterInconsistent`, `HighWaterExhausted`; successful decode does **not** bypass the existing Well-Formedness/checker pipeline; no silent repair. **(H) RevisionIdentity** (ADR-0009 §9) — **not** persisted as authority; recomputed on reload via ADR-0008/N-22-B-1; equivalent canonical content reproduces the same identity. **(I) Local-file integration** (`ordina-desktop`, ADR-0009 §10/§11/§14) — minimum: caller-selected local envelope path, load on launch/use, whole-envelope save, same-directory temp write, flush/`sync_all` before replace, replace-existing atomic/non-torn transition, Windows behavior verified against the accepted invariant (mechanism implementation-chosen, not pinned), old-complete-OR-new-complete target after interruption; OQ-3 stays an implementation/UX detail (minimum only — no path-picker product); **no generic persistence framework**. **(J) Conformance tests** (ADR-0009 §16) — complete Strategy round-trip; opaque-identity preservation; CheckerContext round-trip; huge valid ExactNumber; out-of-domain rational rejection; unknown-format rejection; malformed/truncated input; high-water consistency rejection; restart mint continuity; remove-highest-ID → save → restart → mint → no reuse; `u64::MAX-1`; `u64::MAX`; persisted `Exhausted`; no-wrap/no-reuse; interrupted-save behavior; RevisionIdentity equality after round-trip; Windows replacement invariant where mechanically testable — using the repository's existing test/harness conventions. **Authorized surfaces:** `ordina/src/**` (new `persistence` module; `pub(crate)` `OccurrenceId` accessor and `ExactNumber` reconstruction constructor), `ordina-desktop/src/**` (checked minter advance + `Exhausted`, envelope construction, file I/O, load-on-launch/save), `Cargo.toml`/`Cargo.lock` (serde/serde_json per ADR-0009 §4.4), and `tests`/colocated tests. **Explicitly NOT authorized:** accounts/auth; networking; cloud/sync; multi-user persistence; concurrent writers or locking; server/database persistence; backup/history; generic import/export; generic Strategy editor; installer/updater; telemetry; AI authoring; NOTE-3 resolution; Q-08/N-24/F-8; unrelated refactoring; reopening ADR-0003/0005/0007/0008/0009 or RFC-0004/0005; any Product/architecture decision; **and the implementation itself in this cycle** (this is authorization-only). **STOP rule:** if implementation discovers a Product/architecture question not settled by accepted authority — or would need to weaken an opaque boundary, change semantic identity, or invent persistence semantics — it MUST STOP and report rather than deciding. No `docs/adr/**`/`docs/rfc/**` change authorized. **RFC-0005: ACCEPTED. ADR-0009: ACCEPTED. Persistence implementation: AUTHORIZED — NOT STARTED. Dogfood-0: BLOCKED PENDING PERSISTENCE IMPLEMENTATION (now authorized).** Q-08/N-24/F-8 remain OPEN. Terminates under §12(7).) |
| **PBA-0182** | **ALLOCATED** | **Dogfood-0 Persistence Final-Wiring Remediation authorization** (2026-08-20; explicit Product Owner direction to authorize the bounded remediation required by the **FAILED** independent review of the Dogfood-0 persistence implementation (`9a83f9e`; 0 BLOCKER, 1 MAJOR, 1 MINOR, 2 NOTE; the failed review allocated **no ARR**, matching the established failed-review pattern — cf. PBA-0179 ADR-0009 remediation, ADR-0008 PBA-0170/0171/0172). Governing baseline HEAD `9a83f9e`. The persistence **core** passed every architecture gate; the sole MAJOR is that the correct, tested `ordina::persistence` capability is **not reachable by a human** running `ordina-desktop` (`main.rs` still boots from `correction_fixtures`; `load_envelope`/`save_session`/`resume_session` are test-only). **Scope — remediate exactly the two authorized findings and nothing else, using the existing accepted/implemented APIs (no persistence redesign):** **MAJOR-1 (final desktop wiring)** — make the implemented capability human-reachable: **(A) startup/load** — support a **caller-supplied local envelope path** (CLI arg / env / config; **no** productized file-picker); when an envelope exists, load + `deserialize_envelope` + restore Strategy + restore `CheckerContext` + restore persisted `MintHighWater` + `CorrectionSession::resume` + run the existing checker + recompute `RevisionIdentity` via N-22-B-1 only when Well-Formed; do **not** boot the hard-coded fixture when a real path is supplied. **(B) first run** — when no envelope exists, use only the minimum existing first-run/developer input already permitted; do **not** manufacture/infer/default `DimensionBinding` or supported `VersionToken`; OQ-3 stays an implementation detail. **(C) save** — expose a minimum human-reachable save that calls the existing whole-envelope atomic `save_session` (Strategy + CheckerContext + current `MintHighWater` as **one** envelope; no separate Strategy/high-water save). **(D) restart continuity** — the runnable path must support launch→load→diagnose→edit→confirm→full re-check→recompute RevisionIdentity(WF)→save→exit→relaunch→load→resume→edit→mint collision-free→save, with **no hidden in-memory state** required across restart. **(E) exhausted-corpus behavior** — before any mint-capable operation, check mintability (`MintHighWater`/`can_mint`) and refuse cleanly if `Exhausted`; the `IdentityMinter::next()` panic path must be **unreachable** through normal supported UI use; no wrap/reuse/remint. **(F) fixture handling** — dev fixtures may remain for tests/dev mode, but the real-persistence path must **not** silently fall back to fixture state when a path was supplied, and a failed load of a supplied envelope must be **surfaced**, never replaced with fixture data. **MINOR-1 (failing/interrupted-save test)** — add one bounded conformance test proving an interrupted/failed `atomic_write`/replace leaves the prior valid destination byte-unchanged (pre-create a valid envelope; force the replace to fail; assert `Err`; assert the prior destination is intact and no torn envelope becomes the target); use deterministic failure injection consistent with existing test conventions; do **not** weaken the atomic-save implementation to ease testing. **NOTE-1 disposition:** no core `IdentityMinter` redesign required; the MAJOR-1 wiring must guarantee the `can_mint`/high-water check precedes any minting path so the exhaustion panic is unreachable in supported flow; a small `Result`-based refusal at the wiring boundary is permitted if mechanically cleaner and within the already-accepted exhaustion semantics — **no architecture reopening**. **NOTE-2:** persistence-core affirmations remain closed/non-defect. **Preserved closed implementation (must NOT be reopened; only mechanical integration dependencies may change):** the `ordina::persistence` architecture, JSON+`serde`/`serde_json`, the DTO schema, ExactNumber's semantic domain, the opaque `pub(crate)` transport accessors, high-water derivation, RevisionIdentity encoding (ADR-0008), N-22-B-1, N-22-B-2 correction semantics, ADR-0007's `OccurrenceId` exclusion, and RFC-0005/ADR-0009 decisions. **Architecture hard stop — PASS:** the wiring requires no new Product/architecture decision, no new first-run Product workflow, no new canonical `CheckerContext` source, no persistence-format/opacity/identity/durability change — it composes existing accepted APIs; if implementation nonetheless reveals such a need it MUST STOP and report rather than deciding. **Does NOT authorize:** generic Strategy-editor expansion; productized file-picker/file-browser UX; accounts/auth; networking/cloud/sync; DB; multi-user/concurrency/locking; backup/history; generic import/export; installer/updater; telemetry; AI; NOTE-3 resolution; Q-08/N-24/F-8; unrelated refactoring; any persistence-architecture change; **and the implementation itself in this cycle** (authorization-only). **RFC-0005: ACCEPTED. ADR-0009: ACCEPTED. Persistence core: PRESERVED. Dogfood-0 persistence remediation: AUTHORIZED — NOT STARTED. Dogfood-0 actual human usability: NOT YET READY. Dogfood-0 closure: HOLD.** No ER/ARR/PBR/PBD allocation this cycle, matching the established authorization-only pattern; the remediation ER is allocated when the remediation cycle completes; independent re-review and Dogfood-0 closure are separate subsequent cycles. Q-08/N-24/F-8 remain OPEN. Terminates under §12(7).) |

**MINOR-1** (interrupted/failing-save test) — `interrupted_save_preserves_the_previous_valid_envelope` deterministically forces the temp-file creation to fail before the atomic replace and asserts the prior destination is byte-unchanged, still deserializes, and not torn (real `atomic_write`, no production seam / no weakening). **Prior NOTE-1** (exhaustion) — the `can_mint(session.high_water())` gate wraps the sole editing entry point ("Begin correction"), so `Exhausted` is refused cleanly and the minter's fail-closed panic is unreachable through normal supported UI. **Current findings: 0 BLOCKER, 0 MAJOR, 1 non-blocking MINOR, 2 NOTE.** **MINOR (non-blocking, preserved as a future bounded quality follow-up — NOT remediated here, NOT a closure blocker):** `main::run()`'s startup branch selection (existing→load / absent→first-run / **load-error→no-fixture-fallback**) is correct and manually smoke-verified but lacks automated regression coverage because it reads `std::env::args()` directly; suggested follow-up — extract a pure `resolve_startup(Option<PathBuf>)` helper and add three unit tests. **NOTE-1** (near-`u64::MAX` multi-mint residual — astronomically unreachable, accepted) and **NOTE-2** (interactive GUI Save/edit click-through not mechanically automatable in the review environment; the underlying save/load/resume/continuity/interrupted-save/exhaustion logic is covered by green automated tests) — both non-defect, no action. **Regression + gates PASS (reproduced independently):** `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo build/test --workspace` all clean — 572 lib + 11 + 18 desktop + 13 + 5 + 14 compile-fail, **0 failures**; N-22-A/N-22-B-1/N-22-B-2 intact (correction_loop 13 green). **Persistence core PRESERVED:** commit `0f44bf3` changed only `ordina-desktop/src/{main,app,correction_view,persist}.rs` + the ledger; **no** `ordina/src/**`, `Cargo`, `docs/adr/**`, or `docs/rfc/**` byte change; no new public opaque-handle reader; no persistence-architecture reopening; no excluded scope (no file-picker product / generic editor / accounts / network / DB / etc.). **CLOSURE (recorded in this same ARR under the ARR-only successful-review-closure precedent — cf. ARR-0055 N-22-A, ARR-0059 N-22-B-1):** required architecture ACCEPTED (RFC-0005/ADR-0009); persistence implementation COMPLETE (PBA-0181/ER-0152); independent persistence review completed (FAIL → bounded remediation); final desktop-wiring remediation COMPLETE (PBA-0182/ER-0153); this independent final re-review PASS; a human-use route exists and is launch-verified on Windows; remaining MINOR/NOTEs non-blocking. **Dogfood-0: COMPLETE — CLOSED.** **Dogfood-0 app readiness: READY FOR PRODUCT OWNER USE.** **First real Product Owner Dogfood session (launch → edit → save → close → relaunch → resume): NOT YET PERFORMED** — closure means the product is ready for that session, not that it has occurred; the interactive click-through is the Product Owner's own first live Dogfood confirmation. **Windows readiness: READY.** Q-08/N-24/F-8 remain OPEN. Next permitted action: the Product Owner performs the first real Dogfood-0 session on Windows. Terminates under §12(7).) |

| **ER-0097** | **ALLOCATED** | **ARR-0037 B-1 / B-2 Discriminator-Authority and Atom-Identity Determination** (authority PBA-0115; 2026-08-17; Product Board determination adopted by the Product Owner, recorded under the ER-0092 precedent; confirms accepted text, **no successor revision**. **RULING 1** — §6.4.2's `Used by` entries are **descriptive applicability guidance, not exhaustive assignments**: §6.3 component 3 introduces the same check lists with "**for example**", states the governing entitlement structurally ("where one construct can produce multiple defects of the same category"), and delegates only *kinds and their values* to §6.4.2; each §6.4.2 row additionally carries its own structural applicability clause. **Check 5 is therefore normatively authorized to use `ATOM`** for guard-atom offenders. **RULING 2** — under §3.6 (`guard ::= a finite (possibly empty) conjunction of atoms`) and §4.5 ("Atom order within a guard carries **no meaning**… Two guards with the same atom set are the same guard"), a guard has **set semantics**; content-identical atoms within one guard are **one semantic atom** (`A ∧ A ≡ A`). "One per atom" / "one per offending reference" therefore means **one per distinct atom**: exactly one defect is required, `ATOM(lineage, operator, literal)` **is total** over the semantic atom set, the Revision-4 §C.9 totality proof **stands as written**, and **no positional, ordinal, traversal, or occurrence-based component may enter defect identity** (§6.4, §C.1, §4.5). Duplicate-identical atoms collapse as **canonical-input set semantics before predicate evaluation**, never by deduplicating emitted defect identities, which §6.3 forbids across the qualification boundary. **Dispositions:** ARR-0037 **B-1 CLOSED — NOT A DEFECT** (no code change; the authority is §6.3, which ARR-0037 under-weighted); **B-2 reclassified BLOCKER → MAJOR** incorrect-multiplicity implementation divergence, requiring bounded remediation of `src/checks/configuration_position.rs` only; **m-1 and m-2 carried as test obligations** of that remediation. B-1 and B-2 are logically independent; `src/result/**` is **not** touched; **no Revision 5 required**. Accepted check 2 exhibits the same duplicate-atom divergence prospectively — its acceptance and ARR-0035 **stand**, and it is **explicitly out of scope** and frozen until separately authorized. New observation **O-4** recorded in the Slice-1 implementation plan's existing carried-observations register. Determination-only cycle: no source, test, `Cargo`/toolchain, or accepted-contract change) |

Where multiple independent root-blocker candidates exist for one suppressed unit, exactly one is selected via the **existing, unmodified** `DefectIdentity`/`DefectCore` §6.4 canonical order (`Ord`/`try_cmp`) — the canonical-minimum candidate; no duplicate record is ever emitted for one `(check, construct)`; `NotEvaluatedRecord` is **not** changed to `Vec<DefectIdentity>`. **RULING B (set-membership scope, no new ordering).** The not-evaluated collection's contract-visible identity is record membership, not sequence — obligation 8's "the same... not-evaluated set" is a *set* requirement, and §6.4's canonical total order is textually scoped to "Canonical **defect** ordering" only, never extended to not-evaluated records by any accepted text. A totality proof is added distinguishing semantic encodability (unconditionally total, by construction — CULV encodes every finite non-negative integer) from practical resource availability (an implementation may exhaust memory/time on an absurdly large value, an ordinary runtime failure, not a Product/model representability limit). ### 11.1 Baseline Freeze Record

The pre-baseline synchronization terminates here. Four cycles ran immediately
before and around the first Git baseline. Two were clerical cycles whose sole
purpose was bringing this ledger current; the other two were the pre-Git readiness
review and the baseline establishment itself, recorded here because their
identifiers were allocated in the same period:

| Post-baseline hygiene | PBA-0030 | ER-0038 | Correct §11.1 narrative drift; establish the repository line-ending policy |
| Slice-1 stack analysis | PBA-0031 | ER-0039 | Read-only stack recommendation (no ledger write; recorded by the cycle below, per §12(6)) |

| **ADR-0005 §7.6 HashMap authority-boundary determination** | **PBA-0124** | **ER-0103** | Read-only Product Board determination of the ADR-0005 §7.6 HashMap/HashSet authority-boundary question, adopted by the Product Owner (ER-0092 precedent). **DETERMINATION A — ADR BEHAVIORAL RULE GOVERNS.** ADR-0005 §7.6 is a behavioural determinism rule: it prohibits nondeterministic influence from HashMap/HashSet iteration order on authoritative or conformance-visible results; it does **not** structurally prohibit those collections. **Every identifier in the table above is recorded as an ordinary ALLOCATED entry in
§5 and §8**, including each cycle's own. They are additionally listed here so their
**administrative character** is explicit and so a later reader does not mistake
them for product, architecture, or engineering authorizations.

**Termination.** Recording a synchronization cycle's own identifiers **does not
create an obligation to run another synchronization cycle.** That is the whole
point of this section: the loop stops here, by rule, not by omission. See §12(7).

## 12. Maintenance — the Record-at-Issue Procedure

Every future identifier in an allocated namespace is recorded here **at issue**, not
retrospectively. The procedure below makes that rule operational; it is a
clarification of the existing rule, **not** a new artifact-numbering policy.

1. **Responsibility.** The **Product Board**, or the **Board-authorized executor
   acting for the Board**, is responsible for ensuring that a newly issued
   governance identifier is recorded in this canonical ledger. The obligation
   travels with the authorization; it is never nobody's.
2. **Consult before issuing.** Allocation **MUST** consult the current ledger
   before an identifier is issued. An identifier is never chosen from memory,
   from `max + 1`, or from the apparent shape of a sequence.
3. **Eligibility.** Only an identifier currently eligible under the
   **AVAILABLE-FUTURE** rule (§4) may be allocated. ALLOCATED, RESERVED,
   SUPERSEDED-ID, and UNKNOWN-HISTORICAL identifiers are never reused, and §11.1
   "Pending synchronization" entries are never treated as available.
4. **When to record.** The allocation is recorded **in the same authorized
   governance cycle in which the identifier is issued** — or, where that cycle's
   issuing action is itself read-only, **at the first repository-writing step
   authorized for that cycle**.
5. **Verify before the cycle completes.** The executor confirms that: the
   identifier appears **exactly once** as an active allocation; the namespace
   **high-water mark** is correct; the **next-allocatable** value is correct; and
   **no existing historical assignment was displaced or reused**.
6. **Read-only reviewers do not mutate this ledger.** A reviewer is neither
   required nor permitted to edit the ledger merely because its own authorization
   or report consumes identifiers. Those allocations are carried in §11.1 and
   **must be synchronized by the next authorized repository-writing action, before
   any baseline or publication action.**

7. **Termination rule for ledger-only cycles (baseline freeze).** A governance
   cycle whose **sole purpose** is synchronizing this ledger records **its own**
   authorization and report identifiers **within its own edit**, as ordinary
   ALLOCATED entries, and advances the high-water and next-allocatable values to
   match. Having done so, it is **complete**: recording a synchronization cycle's
   own identifiers **never** obliges a further synchronization cycle. This
   distinguishes (A) identifiers of governance artifacts whose existence must be
   recorded here, from (B) the authorization/report pair of a purely clerical
   ledger-synchronization cycle — both are recorded, but only (A) can generate
   further governance work. This is a termination rule for the pre-baseline
   procedure; it changes no historical numbering policy and creates no exception to
   §12(2) or §12(3).

**Corrections to history.** Any correction to a historical assignment requires an
explicit Product Board determination citing §3(a) or §3(b). Both the superseded and
the corrected identifier are retained in the tables above so the history stays
legible.
